class Solution:
    def minCost(self, n: int, cuts: List[int]) -> int:
        
        cuts = sorted(chain(cuts,[0,n]))
        
        @lru_cache(None)
        def dfs(l, r):
            length, M = cuts[r] - cuts[l], range(l+1, r)
            return min((dfs(l,i) + dfs(i,r) for i in M),
                       default = length) + length
        
        return dfs(0, len(cuts)-1)

A practical pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.
- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

@lru_cache(None)
def dfs(l,r):
    if l + 1 == r:
        return 0
    length = cuts[r] - cuts[l]

cuts = sorted(cuts + [0,n])
@lru_cache(None)
def cost(left, right):
    if right - left == 1:
        return 0
    return min(cost(left, mid) + cost(mid, right) + cuts[right] - cuts[left] for mid in range(left+1, right))

```python
from functools import lru_cache
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minCost(self, n: int, cuts: List[int]) -> int:
        cuts = sorted(cuts + [0, n])

        @lru_cache(None)
        def dfs(l: int, r: int) -> int:
            if r - l == 1:
                return 0
            return min(
                dfs(l, k) + dfs(k, r) + cuts[r] - cuts[l]
                for k in range(l + 1, r)
            )

        return dfs(0, len(cuts) - 1)
```

Let's craft final code:

```
from functools import lru_cache
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minCost(self, n: int, cuts: List[int]) -> int:
        cuts = sorted(set(cuts + [0, n]))

        @lru_cache(None)
        def dfs(l: int, r: int) -> int:
            if r - l <= 1:
                return 0
            return min(
                dfs(l, k) + dfs(k, r) + cuts[r] - cuts[l]
                for k in range(l + 1, r)
            )

        return dfs(0, len(cuts) - 1)
```

Let's think if there is a subtle bug in recurrence with `cuts[r] - cuts[l]` added inside min. In my code:
```
return min(
    dfs(l, k) + dfs(k, r) + cuts[r] - cuts[l]
    for k in range(l + 1, r)
)
```
Operator precedence: `dfs(l,k) + dfs(k,r) + cuts[r] - cuts[l]` is `(dfs + dfs + cuts[r]) - cuts[l]`, same as `dfs + dfs + (cuts[r]-cuts[l])`. Good. But for clarity, use parentheses:
```
dfs(l, k) + dfs(k, r) + (cuts[r] - cuts[l])
```
Better. Use that.

=== RAW ANSWER ===
from functools import lru_cache
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minCost(self, n: int, cuts: List[int]) -> int:
        cuts = sorted(set(cuts + [0, n]))

        @lru_cache(None)
        def dfs(l: int, r: int) -> int:
            if r - l <= 1:
                return 0
            return min(
                dfs(l, k) + dfs(k, r) + (cuts[r] - cuts[l])
                for k in range(l + 1, r)
            )

        return dfs(0, len(cuts) - 1)
